Extra proofs (+5 from Riemannian geometry)

Proof 1: Hopf-Rinow: geodesically complete Riemannian manifold is metrically complete

Statement: Hopf-Rinow: geodesically complete Riemannian manifold is metrically complete

Hypotheses: M connected Riemannian manifold

Conclusion: geodesic complete <=> metric complete <=> closed bounded sets compact

Definitions: Hopf-Rinow, completeness, geodesic

Proof:

Step 1: Geodesic => metric complete (shortest paths exist)

Step 2: Metric complete => closed bounded sets compact

Step 3: Closed bounded compact => Hopf-Rinow

Step 4: Any two points connected by minimizing geodesic

ID: 70e2ac27c57a | Status: UNFORMALIZED | Complexity: O(n)

Mutation test: Equivalence of three notions of completeness

Provenance: Riemannian geometry proof #1

Proof 2: Bochner technique: harmonic forms with nonneg Ricci are parallel

Statement: Bochner technique: harmonic forms with nonneg Ricci are parallel

Hypotheses: Ric >= 0, omega harmonic k-form

Conclusion: Delta omega = 0 and Ric >= 0 => nabla omega = 0

Definitions: Bochner, harmonic, parallel

Proof:

Step 1: Bochner formula: 0 = integral |nabla omega|^2 + Ric terms

Step 2: Both terms nonnegative

Step 3: Both must vanish

Step 4: Therefore nabla omega = 0: omega is parallel

ID: fa7ad60fbb06 | Status: UNFORMALIZED | Complexity: O(n^2)

Mutation test: Topological consequences: b_k determined by curvature

Provenance: Riemannian geometry proof #2

Proof 3: Calabi-Yau theorem: Kähler manifold with c_1=0 has Ricci-flat metric

Statement: Calabi-Yau theorem: Kähler manifold with c_1=0 has Ricci-flat metric

Hypotheses: M compact Kähler, first Chern class c_1(M) = 0

Conclusion: exists unique Ricci-flat Kähler metric in given Kähler class

Definitions: Calabi-Yau, Ricci-flat, Kähler

Proof:

Step 1: Yau's proof of Calabi conjecture

Step 2: Solve Monge-Ampère equation

Step 3: deg(L) > 0 ensures solution

Step 4: Uniqueness by maximum principle

ID: f2b868ac4478 | Status: UNFORMALIZED | Complexity: O(n log n)

Proof 4: Berger classification: simply connected irreducible Riemannian manifolds of positive sectional curvature are classified in even dimensions

Statement: Berger classification: simply connected irreducible Riemannian manifolds of positive sectional curvature are classified in even dimensions

Hypotheses: M simply connected, irreducible, K > 0

Conclusion: limited list: spheres, CP^n, Grassmannians, G2, Spin(7) holonomy

Definitions: Berger classification, holonomy, symmetry

Proof:

Step 1: Holonomy group constrains geometry

Step 2: Berger classified possible holonomy groups

Step 3: Positive curvature severely restricts holonomy

Step 4: Most cases ruled out by direct analysis

Proof 5: Sullivan rigidity: two closed negatively curved manifolds homeomorphic iff isometric (in dim >= 3)

Statement: Sullivan rigidity: two closed negatively curved manifolds homeomorphic iff isometric (in dim >= 3)

Hypotheses: M,N closed, dim >= 3, K < 0

Conclusion: homeomorphic => isometric

Definitions: Sullivan rigidity, Mostow, negative curvature

Proof:

Step 1: Mostow rigidity for hyperbolic manifolds

Step 2: Margulis superrigidity extends

Step 3: Algebraic structure of fundamental group

Step 4: Conjugation in Lie groups determines metric
